**Edition 3.65 software verification:** Complete locked CPU suite passes (360 passed, 16 skipped, 79.67% coverage); Ruff lint/format pass across 188 maintained Python files; mypy passes on 118 configured files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. The repeated-split experiment uses CPU NumPy/SciPy only; the pinned GPU suite and hosted CI were not rerun.

**Edition 3.66 environment-aware static RT cache implementation:** Replaced the previously identity-only P5 cache groundwork with a user-invokable, immutable cache for static Sionna RT datasets. `loslab rt-data --cache-dir PATH` now resolves and probes the available Mitsuba backend, hashes complete scene asset closure and the physical/grid/solver/seed/source/runtime inputs, reuses only matching verified entries, and otherwise generates and atomically publishes the dataset plus a hash/shape/report sidecar. Runtime identity includes OS/release/architecture, Python implementation, CUDA/Mitsuba/Dr.Jit environment selectors, and the visible NVIDIA device/driver fingerprint when available; it degrades deterministically to `unavailable` when `nvidia-smi` is absent. The exact runtime fingerprint is shared between cache preflight and generation. Incomplete scene manifests bypass cache reads and writes. Payload, sidecar, input-key, generation-report, sample-count, and channel-shape checks fail closed; conflicting outputs for the same identity are rejected. Trajectory records retain their run identities but are not yet wired to this static dataset cache. Cache reuse is a software optimization and does not establish cross-device bitwise equivalence.

**Edition 3.66 software verification:** The locked CPU suite passes (369 passed, 16 skipped, 79.91% coverage). Ruff lint and format checks pass across 190 maintained Python files; mypy passes all 119 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Six focused native Sionna backend/LLVM trajectory tests pass in the pinned environment. This WSL host does not provide usable OptiX, so the automatic native probe exercises the configured LLVM fallback; OptiX execution must be validated on the later Linux host that supports it. Hosted CI is intentionally deferred until the implementation batch is ready for a single full review.

**Edition 3.67 label-semantics contract:** Added a first-class `ChannelDataset.label_semantics` field and persisted it in the pickle-free NPZ format, with legacy artifacts loading as `unspecified_legacy`. The TDL/CDL generator labels its binary target `profile_proxy`, the RT generator labels the depth-zero direct-path target `geometric_direct_path_oracle`, and debug fixtures are marked `synthetic_debug_direct_component`. Dataset selection preserves the value, report/field disagreements are rejected, and concatenation rejects incompatible binary targets so a profile proxy cannot silently be scored as geometric LOS. This is a compatibility guard and does not yet provide separate masked geometric, observable-direct, blockage, or continuous path targets; richer target records remain in the typed NR data model and are not retrofitted into the legacy channel classifier.

**Edition 3.67 software verification:** Locked CPU suite passes (373 passed, 16 skipped, 79.94% coverage); Ruff lint/format pass across 190 maintained Python files; mypy passes all 119 configured source files; lock check, package build, and whitespace checks pass. Nine focused native Sionna backend, LLVM trajectory, and standardized TDL/CDL generator tests pass. OptiX remains unavailable on this WSL host and is not claimed as verified.

**Edition 3.68 RT environment diagnostics:** Added opt-in `python -m loslab doctor --probe-rt`. The default doctor command remains a fast version-only report; the opt-in performs the same isolated native probe used by generation and reports requested backend, resolved Mitsuba variant, native versions, probe outcome, and fallback reason in JSON. Unavailable or failed backends are returned as structured diagnostics, so a WSL OptiX failure can be distinguished from a successful LLVM fallback before users spend time generating data. The `rt-data` auto mode continues to select a verified OptiX backend where available and falls back to LLVM otherwise.

**Edition 3.68 verification and local capability result:** The pinned Sionna 2.2 environment ran `python -m loslab doctor --probe-rt`; CUDA variants were installed, but the native CUDA probe failed with `Could not initialize OptiX!`, after which the native direct-path LLVM probe succeeded with `llvm_ad_mono_polarized`. This verifies automatic fallback on this host, not OptiX. A real `box_one_screen` dataset run through `generate_raytraced_cached` produced four balanced geometric-RT samples and a second call loaded the hash-verified entry with identical generation provenance; this is a software integration smoke, not a new research dataset result. The complete locked CPU suite passes (375 passed, 16 skipped, 79.98% coverage); Ruff lint/format pass across 190 maintained Python files; mypy passes all 119 configured files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Focused CLI tests cover default version-only behavior, successful backend reporting, and structured backend-unavailable reporting.

**Edition 3.69 evaluation-label gate:** The experiment runner now resolves one canonical target meaning before creating a run directory or fitting any model. It uses the dataset-level semantic contract for new artifacts, checks recognized per-sample semantics against it, and safely recovers uniform legacy RT/profile/debug labels from row metadata. Mixed or unknown legacy labels stop before training, preventing geometric direct-path labels and TDL/CDL profile proxies from being pooled into a misleading binary score. Run manifests retain both the resolved task meaning and the original per-source semantic description. This protects the legacy binary experiment path; it does not add soft labels or extra target heads.

**Edition 3.69 focused verification:** The experiment-runner tests pass, including positive resolution of uniform legacy RT metadata and a negative check that mixed geometric/profile labels fail before any run directory or model is created. Ruff and mypy pass for the changed implementation. The next complete suite is deferred to the planned consolidated verification batch.

**Edition 3.70 RT target contract:** Each generated RT-to-NR record now carries explicit validity masks for both full-path CFR and geometric LOS truth; the LOS mask is a scalar boolean rather than an implicit absence of masking. Record provenance names each target's semantics and unit, documenting that `channel_cfr` is an absolute-delay full-path complex response on effective PUSCH subcarriers and `geometric_los` is depth-zero LOS-only solver visibility. These values remain private targets and are not added to `NRModelInputs`.

**Edition 3.70 focused verification:** The native LLVM RT trajectory generation/storage round-trip and NR record/trajectory contract tests pass (19 tests); mypy and Ruff pass for the changed implementation. Full-suite verification will be consolidated after the current implementation batch.

**Edition 3.71 legacy-label sentinel handling:** Legacy artifacts that carry `UNSPECIFIED`, `UNKNOWN`, or `UNKNOWN_LEGACY` in per-sample metadata are now treated as unresolved labels, not as a valid task category. A dataset-level declared semantic can still resolve rows lacking old metadata, while explicit contradictory row labels fail. Eight focused experiment-runner tests pass, including consistent old RT-label recovery and prevention of mixed/unknown-label runs before training.

**Edition 3.72 RT trajectory cache:** RT-to-NR generation now computes its complete run identity before scene loading or ray tracing. A matching trajectory-ID manifest loads only after verifying the generation sidecar, identity-key contents, content-addressed trajectory, and each record's embedded identity; this returns without constructing a PathSolver. Reusing a trajectory ID for changed physical/runtime inputs fails before tracing and directs the caller to use a new ID. Interrupted publication without a sidecar is regenerated. Incomplete scene manifests bypass identity reuse. The feature complements the static dataset NPZ cache; cache hits preserve receiver-only observations and private target arrays exactly.

**Edition 3.72 focused verification:** Native LLVM route generation and storage round-trip pass, followed by a second call with PathSolver replaced by a fail-fast stub to prove the cache avoids retracing. A changed-SNR run using the same trajectory ID is rejected before solver construction. All 19 focused native trajectory and NR record-contract tests pass, along with Ruff and mypy for the changed code.

**Edition 3.73 hardware identity:** Extended the NVIDIA runtime fingerprint with GPU index, UUID, PCI bus ID, model, driver, compute capability, and memory size. This distinguishes identical-card hosts/devices more safely when forming CUDA RT cache identities; the environment variables controlling device visibility/order remain included. The CPU/LLVM path remains usable when `nvidia-smi` is unavailable.

**Edition 3.74 typed target definitions:** Added `TargetDefinition(semantic, units, source)` to the typed NR record contract and its pickle-free content-addressed storage, with backward-compatible reads for records created before the field existed. A nonempty definition map must cover every declared target. RT trajectory records define the absolute-delay CFR and depth-zero geometric LOS oracle separately; TDL→PUSCH records define the perfect synchronized channel target and Sionna PHY source. Definitions remain outside the observation-only model input. Dataset NR contract tests pass (27), and native LLVM trajectory plus native TDL→PUSCH tests pass (9); Ruff and mypy pass for changed code.

**Edition 3.75 trajectory consistency:** `NRTrajectory` now rejects changes in the meaning/unit/source definition of a target across time steps, while still allowing legacy records with no typed definitions. A focused contract test exercises definition drift; dataset record tests, Ruff, and mypy pass.

**Edition 3.76 matched baseline expansion:** Added a portable scikit-learn histogram gradient-boosting classifier (`histgb`) to the estimator registry and experiment runner, including immutable model/prediction artifacts and a paired MLP-versus-HistGB seed comparison. It joins the default baseline set beside RF, MLP, CORAL-off, and CORAL; users may also select it alone. Model-factory/fit and end-to-end runner tests pass. This broadens P6 controls but does not yet establish equal tuning budgets, a capacity/compute Pareto study, XGBoost, a CNN/CVNN, or performance superiority.

**Edition 3.77 observation-only CNN baseline:** Added an optional real-valued 1D CNN that consumes the same complex receiver observation as per-link real and imaginary channels over ordered subcarriers. Channel-wise scaling is fitted only on training observations; validation cross-entropy controls early stopping and the decision threshold remains validation-only. Checkpoints use tensor-only loading, and predictions, split identities, and training diagnostics use the common experiment artifact path. The runner supports paired CNN/MLP, RF/CNN, and HistGB/CNN seed comparisons. CNN is opt-in (`--methods cnn`) because PyTorch remains an optional dependency; portable default CPU experiments continue to run without it. This is an implementation control only: architecture/compute matching, tuning-budget equality, CVNN controls, adequate independent scene counts, and scientific performance evidence remain open.

**Edition 3.77 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (387 passed, 16 optional skips, 79.66% coverage). Ruff lint and formatting pass across 193 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 121 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. CNN tests cover a short CPU fit, safe checkpoint round-trip including the validation threshold, malformed labels/inputs, unavailable CUDA, and raw-complex routing in the experiment runner. No native Sionna code changed in this batch; previous LLVM backend verification remains recorded in edition 3.68. These checks establish software behavior, not scientific performance.

**Edition 3.78 phase-equivariant CVNN control:** Added an optional complex-valued convolutional baseline. Complex convolution uses paired real/imaginary kernels without additive complex bias; modReLU preserves global-phase equivariance, and a magnitude feature head makes binary scores invariant to a common phase rotation. Per-link RMS scaling is learned from training samples only. Validation-only early stopping and threshold selection, tensor-only checkpoints, run artifacts, and paired CVNN-versus-MLP/CNN/RF/HistGB comparisons use the shared runner. The baseline is opt-in because PyTorch remains optional. This tests a precise architectural symmetry on the supplied CFR input; it does not establish calibrated carrier-phase robustness, equal compute/parameter budgets, scientific superiority, or invariance to per-antenna phase errors.

**Edition 3.78 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (391 passed, 16 optional skips, 79.95% coverage). Ruff lint and format pass across 196 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 123 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. CVNN tests verify common-phase-invariant logits, a short CPU training and safe checkpoint round trip, malformed-input rejection, and raw-complex runner routing. This establishes implementation behavior only; compute matching and empirical performance remain open.

**Edition 3.79 baseline resource reporting:** Each experiment result now records estimator-fit wall time, validation and test prediction wall times with sample counts, learned parameter count when available, and peak allocated CUDA memory for Torch methods on CUDA. Neural parameter counts come from the fitted model; sklearn MLP reports learned weights and biases, while estimators without a meaningful parameterization report `null`. Device synchronization brackets measured Torch operations. This makes resource cost reviewable in the immutable results artifact but does not enforce equal budgets or create capacity–performance Pareto sweeps; those remain P6 work.

**Edition 3.79 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (391 passed, 16 optional skips, 79.99% coverage). Ruff lint and formatting pass across 196 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 123 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Runner coverage verifies nonnegative timing fields, MLP learned-parameter accounting, and null CUDA-memory reporting on CPU. Measurements are machine/run specific and are not evidence of matched compute.

**Edition 3.80 capacity controls:** Added named `small`, `medium`, and `large` width presets shared by the sklearn MLP, CORAL MLP, real CNN, and phase-equivariant CVNN. `loslab experiment --capacity` and experiment configs select a tier; the resolved configuration records it and checkpoints preserve it, while older neural checkpoints load as `medium`. Non-neural classical estimators keep their own fixed hyperparameters. This enables reproducible neural capacity sweeps across separate immutable runs; it does not itself equalize FLOPs, optimization updates, search budgets, or classical-versus-neural costs.

**Edition 3.80 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (394 passed, 16 optional skips, 80.06% coverage). Ruff lint and formatting pass across 198 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 124 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Tests assert monotonic parameter counts across capacity presets, preset validation, sklearn MLP architecture selection, and capacity-preserving neural checkpoint round trips. This validates selectable capacity controls, not a completed capacity-performance sweep.

**Edition 3.81 immutable capacity sweep:** Added `scripts/run_capacity_sweep.py` and `experiments/capacity_sweep.py`. One invocation runs requested methods over small/medium/large tiers with identical input hashes, grouped seed splits, observation settings, and device; verifies split hashes across all child runs; and writes per-tier AUROC, parameter-count and training-time points plus child-manifest hashes. Child runs and the aggregate receive completion markers, and existing output paths are never overwritten. A failed run preserves inspectable child artifacts and marks the sweep failed. The report explicitly limits inference to the supplied dataset and states that timing is machine-specific; this runner does not claim budget matching or scene-level evidence.

**Edition 3.81 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (395 passed, 16 optional skips, 80.10% coverage). Ruff lint and formatting pass across 201 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 126 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. The pilot is a software fixture, not research evidence.

**Edition 3.83 optional XGBoost control:** Added an XGBoost tree baseline with deterministic histogram training, single-worker execution, and training-partition-derived binary class weighting. The `boosting` extra uses the CPU-only `xgboost-cpu` distribution, avoiding the default GPU distribution's large NCCL dependency; the neural CI job installs this extra and runs its tests alongside PyTorch coverage. Portable core environments remain free of the optional dependency.

**Edition 3.84 raw-observation MLP:** Added `raw_mlp`, which flattens the receiver-observed CFR's real and imaginary parts without using engineered PDP/magnitude features. It shares train-only sklearn standardization, the named capacity presets, grouped split IDs, predictions, and cost records. The default experiment and capacity-sweep method lists now include it; the existing `mlp` remains the engineered-feature control. Paired comparisons cover raw-vs-feature MLP and raw MLP against RF/HistGB/CNN/CVNN. This closes an input-modality mismatch in C0–C3's implementation; it does not equalize training budgets or establish performance.

**Edition 3.84 focused verification:** Runner tests verify the raw MLP receives flattened real/imaginary receiver values at the expected feature dimension and emits normal immutable artifacts. Full-suite and quality-gate results are pending for this edition.

**Edition 3.84 software verification:** The complete locked CPU suite passes (398 passed, 16 optional skips, 80.11% coverage). Ruff lint and formatting pass across 201 maintained Python files; mypy passes across 126 configured source files; `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. The raw-input test verifies flattened Re/Im dimension and complete prediction artifacts. No performance claim is made.

**Edition 3.85 unknown-order sparse multipath baseline:** Added a receiver-input-only estimator using delay-grid OMP initialization, continuous least-squares delay refinement, a zero-path candidate, and BIC model selection with residual noise variance estimated from observed pilots. The estimator returns an all-tone CFR prediction but does not receive unobserved CFR values, path count, oracle delays, or true noise variance. A seeded 24-cell stress sweep (2 bandwidths × 3 SNRs × 4 true path counts × 40 trials) selected the exact order in 791/960 trials (82.40%); by true order 0/1/2/3, accuracy was 94.58%/91.25%/81.25%/62.50%. This is synthetic algorithm stress evidence only; close/coherent paths, diffuse residuals, receiver-estimator error, and RT/measurement transfer are not covered. The report is `docs/research/results/multipath-order-stress-seed20261022.json` (file SHA256 `98e84f75bf10d0249644e81af9125835b0a0aadbb61a72c82fc54ff1f8e46a28`, internal result digest `41541c7a23c838aee5725fc16437383d525aff83a5b9a59a0489ac90b8e8927c`); reproduce it with `uv run --no-sync --locked python scripts/run_multipath_order_stress.py <new-output.json>`. Source revision: `ed23c66568d2ebae69f9e290745d700bd60b591a`.

**Edition 3.86 runnable NR MVP path and status audit:** Added `loslab nr-observe` to convert compatible saved RT CFR datasets into Sionna PUSCH/DMRS LS observations, preserve group IDs and canonical label semantics, record receiver settings, and refuse output overwrite. Updated the quickstart with `rt-data → nr-observe → experiment`; added `docs/research/MVP_STATUS.md` to define the simulation-only software MVP and its exit checklist. A local CUDA run converted 40 links from the saved `simple_street_canyon` pilot to `[40, 1, 72]` observations with `geometric_direct_path_oracle` semantics and ran the classifier pipeline; the one-scene, one-seed logistic AUROC was 0.50 and is only a plumbing check. The MVP remains incomplete pending multi-scene generation/audit, a frozen independent scene-family test, matched baselines on the DMRS observations, clean-Linux reproduction, and final hosted CI. Local verification: 409 passed, 16 skipped, 80.41% coverage; Ruff check/format pass across 206 Python files; mypy passes all 129 configured files; lock check, package build, and diff checks pass. The latest code has not yet been pushed, so hosted CI is pending.

**Edition 3.87 six-scene DMRS MVP check and BIC numerical stability:** Converted six balanced saved Sionna RT scene datasets (40 links each) through the PUSCH/DMRS adapter into 240 receiver observations of shape `[240, 1, 72]` at configured 12 dB SNR, seed 20261022, with ideal synchronization. Ran logistic regression, random forest, and raw-I/Q MLP on the same observation set with `rt_etoile` held out as the test-only scene (40 samples/20 groups); the remaining five scenes were the only train/validation domains. Three fixed seeds yielded mean test AUROCs 0.334/0.340/0.739 respectively. These small simulation-only results are retained without a superiority claim; they are not a stable benchmark. Source NPZ hashes, DMRS observation hash, run manifest/results/summary hashes, split identity, runtime, and exact commands are recorded in `docs/research/results/mvp-six-scene-dmrs-holdout-etoile-seed20261022.json`. The report marks model-level calibration as unimplemented and keeps scene/material qualification and measured validation out of MVP scope. Separately, CI exposed platform-dependent overfitting in exact noiseless multipath fits: floating-point residual differences caused BIC to select an extra path. The sparse estimator now floors RSS at machine epsilon times observed signal energy, so the score cannot interpret numerical roundoff as physical noise reduction. The full pinned Linux/GPU suite passes locally (409 passed, 16 skipped, 80.32%); Ruff check/format and mypy (129 files) pass. GitHub Actions run [39684428037](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39684428037) passed all four jobs (build, Python 3.11 core, Python 3.12 core, and Python 3.12 neural/boosting) on a clean hosted Linux checkout. The simulation-only software MVP gate is complete. The native Mitsuba LLVM/Dr.Jit backend is already working here, so no standalone LLVM distribution download is needed; runtime auto-probe retains OptiX as the alternate path when its native trace succeeds. All independent-measurement, material qualification, broad standards, receiver impairment, scientific performance, forecasting, and downstream utility gates remain open.

**Edition 3.88 six-fold leave-one-scene-out DMRS evaluation:** Reused the immutable 240-link six-scene Sionna RT→PUSCH/DMRS observation artifact (40 links/scene, `[240,1,72]`, configured 12 dB, ideal synchronization) and ran all six scene-family holdouts, each with logistic regression, RF, and raw-I/Q MLP over seeds 11/23/47/59/71/83/97. Every fold had one test-only scene (40 samples, 4–20 spatial groups) and five disjoint train/validation scenes. Scene-macro mean AUROC was 0.5598 (logistic), 0.5536 (RF), and 0.7817 (raw MLP), with across-scene standard deviations 0.1021/0.1054/0.0802 and ranges 0.3475–0.6586/0.3271–0.6479/0.7154–0.9518. These descriptive summaries treat scene as the aggregation unit; seed repeats do not inflate the scene count. They show meaningful scene heterogeneity and are not evidence of generalization to real or independently qualified environments, nor a claim of method superiority. `scripts/summarize_rt_dmrs_losso.py` verifies exactly one fold per declared scene, common observation hashes/configuration, complete method×seed coverage, identical per-seed split hashes across methods, and exclusion of each held-out scene from training. It also verifies the upstream source-data report references the same DMRS observation hash. Tests cover successful scene-macro aggregation, leakage rejection, and upstream evidence linkage. Input/per-fold hashes and limitations are in `docs/research/results/mvp-six-scene-dmrs-losso-seed20261022.json`; source scene NPZ hashes are cross-linked to the committed DMRS generation report. The local suite passes (412 passed, 16 skipped, 80.42% coverage); Ruff and mypy pass on 208 maintained files and 130 configured source files, respectively. The six-scene LOSO workflow closes the MVP-scale P3 implementation gate, but independent geometry/material qualification, more links per scene, channel impairment sensitivity, measured evidence, and all broader scientific gates remain open. Hosted CI for the new summarizer/tests is pending.

**Edition 3.89 realized path complexity and class-conditional RT outcome accounting:** Audited the 240 accepted records across six balanced RT scenes using the legacy per-row depth-zero geometry labels and path metadata. All 120 accepted geometric-LOS samples were LOS-plus-indirect (20/20 in each scene); all 120 accepted geometric-NLOS samples had indirect full-solver paths (20/20 in each scene). No accepted direct-only LOS or no-path NLOS samples appear, consistent with export/filtering behavior rather than proving those mechanisms are absent. Across candidate full-solver calls, 38/278 (13.7%) returned no valid paths; scene-specific empty fractions ranged from 0 to 24.5%. The legacy v1 reports do not attribute those empty outcomes by geometric class, and empty-path candidates are excluded from channel NPZs, so their class-conditional rate cannot be recovered from these artifacts. For accepted LOS-mixed samples, per-scene median direct-to-indirect power ratios ranged from 0.44 to 9.96 dB; distributions are reported per scene, not pooled as if links were independent. The immutable input hashes, generation counters, path-count histograms, ratio distributions, limitations, and reproduction command are in `docs/research/results/rt-six-scene-label-complexity-audit-20261022.json` (SHA256 `af20ab2d3d57a189dc56997944c6d369c411d10ab4b4e8afd978cef18cc05c8b`). `integrations/sionna/adapter.py` now records full-solver attempts/errors/empty paths/direct-only rejections/CFR failures/acceptances by geometric class and checks class/global accounting invariants; mock tests exercise the new report. This enables the next generation to measure label-conditional acceptance without selection concealment, but does not backfill the legacy runs or close E07. These six synthetic scenes are not independently qualified, and measured blockage/outage evidence remains open. Local verification after these changes: 414 passed, 16 skipped, 80.38% coverage; Ruff passes across 210 maintained files; mypy passes across 131 configured files; build and lock checks pass. Hosted CI for these changes is pending.

**Edition 3.90 native class-conditional RT report smoke:** Ran the updated generator against Sionna RT's `box_one_screen` scene using backend `auto`; the native OptiX probe failed in this WSL environment and the native LLVM/Dr.Jit trace succeeded. The small run accepted four geometric-LOS and four geometric-NLOS samples after 14 geometry-oracle candidates (six class-quota skips) and eight full-solver calls. The new by-class report recorded four calls/four accepts and zero empty/error/rejection outcomes in each class; all class/global totals reconciled. This validates report production on native Sionna RT, not a noncoverage estimate: no empty paths occurred in only eight solver calls. Exact settings, runtime, asset and NPZ hashes are in `docs/research/results/rt-class-conditional-outcome-accounting-native-smoke-20261022.json` (SHA256 `ac7abb70778962f43582ace5a23c4670ca2dd13a8af63c1c0fb5fbcb5454d592`). The broader six-scene audit and E07 limitations in edition 3.89 remain unchanged.

**Edition 3.91 Etoile class-conditional solver noncoverage retrace:** Re-ran the exact six-scene Etoile generation configuration (seed 1021, 5,000,000 samples/source, depth 2, 20 accepted samples/class, same geometry, carrier, grid, and LLVM variant) with the class-conditional outcome counters. All 20 geometric-LOS candidates reached a valid full solution and were accepted as LOS-plus-indirect under the configured selection rule. Of 28 geometric-NLOS candidates, 8 (28.57%) returned no full-solver paths and were excluded; the other 20 had indirect paths and were accepted. There were no solver errors, CFR conversion failures, or LOS-direct-only rejections. The retrace preserved the legacy dataset's labels, group IDs, and direct/full path counts exactly; channel arrays differed only by relative RMS `3.17e-8` (maximum absolute delta relative to the legacy channel peak `1.05e-7`). This checks repeatability and attributes the previously unstratified eight empty outcomes to geometric NLOS candidates for this one selected scene/configuration. Empty solver outcomes are model/budget noncoverage, not measured outage or proof of no physical path. The retrace report records complete runtime, config, both NPZ hashes, and reproduction command at `docs/research/results/rt-etoile-class-conditional-outcome-retrace-20261022.json` (SHA256 `4067f5235d8be85c44c8fec5d8bfe69082d784ad1645ccad26404af22a8e816d`); E07 remains open across the other scenes and solver budgets. The native smoke, generator counters, audit tests, and report were included in hosted CI run [39687228614](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39687228614), which passed all four jobs.


**Edition 3.92 six-scene class-conditional RT noncoverage audit:** Extended the matched LLVM/Dr.Jit retrace to all six scene configurations and compared each retrace with the corresponding legacy adaptive-run dataset. The 240 accepted records retained identical labels and group IDs in all six scenes; CFR arrays were exactly equal in canyon and three urban scenes, and differed only at relative RMS 9.84e-8 (box) and 3.17e-8 (Etoile) in the other two. Across 278 full-solver calls, all 38 empty-path outcomes occurred in geometric-NLOS candidates: 0/120 geometric-LOS calls and 38/158 geometric-NLOS calls (24.05%); no solver errors, direct-only rejections, or CFR conversion failures occurred. Scene-specific geometric-NLOS empty counts were box 0/20, canyon 0/20, Etoile 8/28, urban_00041 9/29, urban_00042 8/28, and urban_00043 13/33. This is a retrospective attribution for the six selected synthetic configurations only. Empty-path outcomes denote configured RT solver/model noncoverage, not measured outage or proof that no physical propagation path exists; the accepted LOS sampling rule requires an indirect path and does not estimate direct-only LOS prevalence. The report includes runtime/backend, scene-asset and dataset hashes, per-scene settings/counters, legacy comparison and limitations at `docs/research/results/rt-six-scene-class-conditional-outcome-retrace-20261022.json` (SHA256 `a59a3714b0fa209849cedd7cf9f9616ac7f0a2ee123c5d256f1ace56c4c8af88`). E07 remains open for solver-budget/depth/seed sensitivity, candidate selection effects, blocked/outage and diffuse cases, independently qualified materials/geometries, and measured validation.


**Edition 3.93 six-scene neural capacity LOSO experiment:** Ran the planned small/medium/large capacity comparison on the locked 240-observation Sionna PUSCH/DMRS artifact with all six scene families held out once, methods `mlp`, `raw_mlp`, `cnn`, `cvnn`, `coral`, and `coral0`, seven fixed seeds, and CUDA-backed neural training. The auditable suite contains 756 fits. `scripts/summarize_capacity_loso.py` verifies each fold/report/child manifest hash, the actual observation artifact hash, complete method×seed×capacity coverage, held-out-scene exclusion from training, and unchanged grouped split identities across capacity tiers; tests exercise aggregation and reject changed inputs/splits. The summary aggregates seed means by scene before computing six-scene macro statistics. Scene-macro AUROC for small/medium/large tiers was MLP 0.524/0.552/0.548, raw-I/Q MLP 0.782/0.792/0.806, CNN 0.791/0.800/0.815, CVNN 0.546/0.530/0.535, CORAL 0.506/0.544/0.550, and CORAL-off 0.506/0.546/0.551. Large versus small improved the per-scene mean in 5/6 scenes for engineered MLP and CORAL variants, 4/6 for raw MLP and CNN, while CVNN improved in 2/6; only six selected scene families are the aggregation units. These descriptive differences do not establish significance or superiority: the holdout contains 40 links and 4–20 spatial groups per scene, scene assets are not independently qualified, architecture training/tuning compute is not equalized, and no measured data are included. Exact configuration, paired deltas, per-scene scores, input and run hashes, and limitations are recorded in `docs/research/results/mvp-capacity-losso-20261022.json` (SHA256 `76e26a4067e3d96b8d99c61a99dc289f6ea85bb9b6a1b2f6feb18dbe3fba7a0c`).


**Edition 3.94 Etoile RT depth and ray-budget sensitivity:** Extended the class-conditional audit on one complex synthetic scene with seed 1021, 20 accepted links/class, depths 1/2/3, and 500k/1M/5M samples per source, plus a second seed (1022) at depth 2 and 5M. At each depth, candidate calls and class-conditional empty-path counts were identical over the three ray budgets for seed 1021. Increasing depth reduced geometric-NLOS empty outcomes from 26/46 (depth 1) to 8/28 (depth 2) to 3/23 (depth 3); geometric-LOS remained 0/20. At depth 2/5M the second seed produced 5/25 NLOS empty calls, compared with 8/28 for seed 1021, illustrating that the single-seed rate is not stable. Receiver locations, labels, groups, and depth-zero direct counts matched across budgets within each depth, but accepted path support and channel estimates did not: versus 5M, relative CFR L2 ranged from 0.02% to 7.07% across depths/budgets; depth-3 full path counts differed for 21/40 accepted records at 500k and 11/40 at 1M. The budget deltas were nonmonotonic, and depth 3 is not established as ground truth. This is a one-scene solver sensitivity diagnostic, not a general convergence threshold or measured outage result. Exact run/data hashes, class outcomes, path-count comparisons, environment, settings, and limitations are in `docs/research/results/rt-etoile-budget-depth-class-outcomes-20261022.json` (SHA256 `0fae0f73353a989366cfbb1b351d0b328bbfbf0396696089f5d34f923ccddd00`). Broader scene, seed, and material qualification remains open.


**Edition 3.95 second-scene RT depth/ray-budget sensitivity:** Repeated the class-conditional outcome sweep on generated `urban_00043` (seed 43001, 20 accepted links/class) at max depths 1/2/3 and 20k/100k/500k samples per source, then added seed 43002 at depth 2/500k. For seed 43001, geometric-NLOS empty-path outcomes fell from 175/195 calls (89.7%) at depth 1 to 13/33 (39.4%) at depth 2 and 3/23 (13.0%) at depth 3; geometric-LOS outcomes were 0/20 at every depth. At each depth, the three tested ray budgets yielded identical class-conditional call/empty counts, while accepted CFR differences and path-count changes generally decreased toward the 500k comparison endpoint. Seed 43002 reproduced the depth-2 NLOS count of 13/33 for this selected setup, whereas the independent Etoile second seed differed from its first run (5/25 vs 8/28). These small fixed-geometry samples support only a configuration-specific depth sensitivity observation, not a universal depth rule or physical outage conclusion. Exact settings, runtime and scene-asset hashes, ten NPZ hashes, per-class counters, accepted-channel comparisons, and limitations are recorded in `docs/research/results/rt-urban43-budget-depth-class-outcomes-20261022.json` (SHA256 `aa4559b8452bff6103d15b30ceac56bb5063765fc7e2c0aacac0f23d5034980f`). The remaining scenes, seeds, ray budgets, and material qualification are open.


**Edition 3.96 measured 5G NR data source screen:** Screened public measurement sources against the actual target definitions rather than treating every 5G dataset as interchangeable. ETH Zurich CAEZ-5G provides measured multi-O-RU PUSCH CSI, noise estimates, and timestamp-aligned UE positions, making it a plausible external source for T16/T17 reconstruction or trajectory prediction after schema and leakage audits. It does not provide geometric LOS/NLOS truth; position alone cannot establish a link label, so it cannot close T01 or the LOS/NLOS portion of E14. The CAEZ license requires attribution, prohibits redistribution of the raw data and subsets, and states use is at the user's risk; download is pending the user's explicit license acceptance. Coverage@CS-KAU provides measured SSB-derived RSRP/RSRQ/RSSI/SINR across mapped indoor points, useful only for link-quality targets and not complex CSI/path labels. Candidate sources, intended targets, limits, and admission rules are recorded in `docs/research/MEASURED_DATA_CANDIDATES.md`. E14 remains open; no measurement has been downloaded, analyzed, or claimed as validation.

**Edition 3.97 cross-scene propagation-depth sensitivity:** Extended the class-conditional full-solver outcome audit across the six current Sionna RT scenes at maximum depths 1/2/3. Across the urban scenes, geometric-NLOS empty-path counts were 113/133 → 9/29 → 2/22 (urban41), 183/203 → 8/28 → 3/23 (urban42), and 175/195 → 13/33 → 3/23 (urban43); Etoile was 26/46 → 8/28 → 3/23. Box and canyon had zero empty-path outcomes in 20/20 NLOS calls at every tested depth. No geometric-LOS sample had an empty-path outcome. This supports a configuration-specific solver-depth sensitivity warning for urban scenes; it does not establish physical outage probabilities or justify a universal depth recommendation. Sample count is 20 accepted examples per geometric class and scene, with scene-specific ray budgets. Exact source artifacts, hashes, seeds, budgets, class-conditional counts, semantics, and limitations are recorded in `docs/research/results/rt-cross-scene-depth-sensitivity-20261022.json` (SHA256 `ca0399a08f0465f4d3ce79a8ed51f758de89346e09e12bc64f366b17c497b9cc`).

**Edition 3.97 CAEZ license and measured-data acquisition:** The user explicitly accepted the CAEZ Dataset License v1.0 and authorized a local research download on 2026-10-22. The official indoor preprocessed downsampled-OFDM magnitude CSI file (5,957,915,148 bytes) is being downloaded into Git-ignored `data/external/caez/`; this avoids the 394 GB raw archive for the first evidence pass. The preprocessed product discards complex phase, and CAEZ provides position rather than LOS/NLOS ground truth, so the planned use is limited to targets supported by audited fields (for example, grouped temporal/trajectory channel-feature prediction), not phase-sensitive reconstruction or geometric LOS classification. The file is not admitted to experiments until transfer integrity, NPZ schema, label/time alignment, provenance, and leakage-safe split design are verified. Raw and derived CAEZ content must remain non-redistributable and outside all Git/public artifacts; E14 remains open.

**Edition 3.98 pickle-free CAEZ schema audit tooling:** Added `scripts/audit_caez_npz.py` and focused tests. The tool verifies ZIP CRCs and the archive SHA256, maps stored numeric NPY members directly with bounded memory, checks required CSI/noise/time/position fields and finite values, and never unpickles object-valued O-RU metadata. The official 500-sample schema subset passes; it reports `[N,4,4,273]` float32 magnitude CSI, `[N,4]` noise estimates, `[N]` timestamps, and `[N,2]` positions. This verifies only file/schema handling, not model quality or LOS/NLOS labels. The full indoor archive remains in transfer and must pass the same audit before use. Targeted tests, Ruff, formatting, and mypy pass for the audit tooling.

**Edition 3.99 measured CSI magnitude forecasting runner:** Added `scripts/evaluate_caez_magnitude_forecast.py` with a strict chronological tail holdout, matched one-second future target alignment, and persistence versus 500 ms linear-trend baselines. It evaluates the four O-RU magnitude tensors in bounded chunks and reports per-O-RU RMSE/MAE plus normalized RMSE; it re-audits archive CRC/SHA/schema and keeps all sample values local. Synthetic tests verify the split boundary and that the trend baseline wins on a deterministic linear fixture. The evaluation is a measured feature-forecasting side task, not LOS/NLOS classification, complex-CSI reconstruction, cross-scene generalization, or evidence of learned-model advantage. Full CAEZ results are pending transfer and audit.

**Edition 3.100 CAEZ measured-data audit and pilot:** Downloaded the official indoor preprocessed archive locally after user license acceptance. The full 5,957,915,148-byte file passes ZIP CRC validation and the pickle-free numeric audit; SHA256 is `706e710cc30a96bbe4386e705d9361497e9c2e5df13c00fba77d9318e915c2cc`. It contains 338,980 finite samples with magnitude CSI `[N,4,4,273]`, four O-RU noise estimates, strictly increasing timestamps, and aligned position fields. A chronological final-20% forecast evaluation with a fixed 1-second horizon and 500 ms lookback scored 67,717 pairs: persistence macro normalized RMSE 0.7082, linear trend 1.4782; persistence is better on each O-RU. These are magnitude-only outputs, not LOS/NLOS labels, not a learned model, and not an independent-site validation. Aggregate provenance/audit is in `docs/research/results/caez-indoor-audit.json` (SHA256 `ca241335c191af74aead8bf5fbc3adfcd688ab8f099c7a190f6a705274741285`) and scores in `docs/research/results/caez-indoor-magnitude-forecast.json` (SHA256 `06badf463569597c87ab247948e104b6c51eda96f70b005ae5a360c50bb9d143`). The raw CAEZ archive and all subsets remain local and ignored by Git; attribution is required for any public report.

The target/label distinctions and the roles of RT, TDL/CDL, analytical, and measured channels are summarized in `docs/research/OUTPUT_TASKS_AND_CHANNELS.md`.


**Edition 3.101 CAEZ compact measured-channel regression:** Extended the audited CAEZ pilot with a fixed StandardScaler+Ridge regressor (alpha=1) for the per-O-RU, per-Rx-antenna subcarrier-mean CSI magnitude in dB. The chronological split uses 237,235 training pairs with target samples before 70%, a 10% unused time gap, and 67,717 final-block test pairs; the one-second horizon uses current and nearest-500-ms past summaries. On the compact target, macro RMSE normalized by each O-RU's held-out target standard deviation is 0.807 for Ridge, 1.047 for persistence, and 2.577 for linear trend; Ridge has lower RMSE than persistence on each of four O-RUs. The distinct full [O-RU,Rx,subcarrier] magnitude-tensor result remains persistence 0.7082 vs. trend 1.4782 normalized by target energy, so the learned result does not establish full-tensor CSI prediction, phase reconstruction, LOS/NLOS inference, or cross-environment transfer. This is a single indoor campaign exploratory pilot; alpha is fixed, not tuned. Updated focused tests verify the compact regression output and chronological partitions. Result JSON SHA256: 0d892aebd2fd18438cf885011fc1598ffe987624f5a80b0de0f3d4b5a2b7cb6c.


**Edition 3.102 CAEZ cross-environment transfer pipeline (measured result pending):** Added a bounded-memory, pickle-free evaluator that audits each official CAEZ archive, trains a fixed StandardScaler+Ridge model only on indoor forecast targets before the 70% time boundary, leaves a 10% source-time gap, and evaluates the final 20% of indoor/outdoor/hallway campaigns without target-domain fitting. Persistence and linear-trend baselines receive the same current and prior measured CSI summaries; positions, labels, and path metadata are not model inputs. The report records archive and code hashes, runtime versions, sample ranges, per-O-RU errors, and paired 10-second time-block bootstrap intervals. Synthetic tests cover source/target split boundaries, matched baseline comparison, feature-shape validation, and deterministic paired bootstrap behavior. The official outdoor and hallway archives are downloaded locally under the already accepted CAEZ license; no cross-environment result is claimed until both pass CRC, schema, and timestamp checks and the frozen evaluator runs successfully. CAEZ provides no geometric LOS/NLOS labels, so this gate can only produce measured magnitude-forecast evidence.

**Edition 3.103 completed CAEZ three-campaign forecast pilot:** Executed the frozen indoor-trained StandardScaler+Ridge evaluator against chronological tail splits from the official indoor, outdoor, and hallway magnitude archives. The one-second compact mean-magnitude target has macro normalized RMSE 0.807/1.047/2.577 (Ridge/persistence/trend) indoors, 0.800/0.915/2.035 outdoors, and 0.945/0.275/0.568 in the hallway. Paired 10-second time-block bootstrap CIs for Ridge minus persistence are [-0.258,-0.222], [-0.131,-0.102], and [+0.445,+0.618], respectively. The model transfers better than persistence on the first two campaigns and loses in the hallway; this is heterogeneous exploratory evidence, not a generalization claim. Inputs are current and prior observed target-domain CSI summaries shared with persistence/trend; no target labels or fitting are used. The separate full-tensor indoor baseline still favors persistence over linear trend. All archives pass CRC/schema and strictly increasing timestamp checks; observed maximum gaps are 0.04/0.14/5.515 seconds (indoor/outdoor/hallway). Local counts (338,980/303,172/290,180) differ from the current ETH page (338,981/303,189/288,180); this is unresolved and must be reconciled against exact file/version before publication. Campaign, UE, and protocol are confounded; intervals are within-campaign only; positions are not LOS/NLOS truth. Report and code hashes, splits, and metrics are in docs/research/results/caez-cross-environment-summary-forecast.json (report SHA256 2697498a6309a4c6fe16a8d11b3e397ae5db62b2bd604558c45d0b79726dd345). Reproduction command and license/source caveats are in docs/research/MEASURED_DATA_CANDIDATES.md. This closes the evaluator execution task but not E14, independent measurement validation, or the broader paper gates.

**Edition 3.104 PSS/SSS continuous-timing operating study and live-status reconciliation:** The PSS/SSS receiver now searches integer timing plus a continuous fractional-sample offset and refines CFO from the PSS cyclic prefix. A fixed-seed operating study calibrated joint score thresholds on 1,024 noise captures and evaluated them on 2,048 independent noise captures; 3/2,048 were accepted as false alarms, with the Wilson upper bound under the configured 5% target. Across 256 trials per SNR/channel, correct cell/timing/CFO acquisition rates at 0/6/12/18 dB were 59/119/206/254 for the flat channel and 54/86/190/250 for the static 12-sample echo channel. A paired comparison against the prior coarse-fractional-grid treatment found the echo-channel acquisition-rate change to be +6.25/+6.64/+24.61/+36.72 percentage points; Holm-adjusted exact McNemar p-values were 0.0051/0.0382/1.72e-12/2.10e-24. The flat-channel gain was smaller; its low-SNR p-values did not survive family correction. These are controlled synthetic results for one four-symbol PSS/SSS fixture, two channel cases, a fixed CFO grid, and one timing range; low-SNR failures remain and this is not field or standards-complete receiver evidence. Operating report SHA256 is 6ac0ee34a97795d6dda3b6e799ad87802d5f742463475753344b0664b7d94f64; paired report SHA256 is e5328063e85f86b37723b4c723471ccbf37856fe37f9cefd9c6972868b12ac68. The live gate matrix above is reconciled with later PSS/SSS and CAEZ results; old verification snapshots elsewhere in this plan remain historical. P2 remains open for interference, channel evolution, broader multipath/CFO/timing, and measured receiver qualification.

**Edition 3.105 3D scene-geometry and endpoint QA:** Added `scripts/render_rt_endpoint_positions.py` and a locked optional `plots` extra. The renderer validates geometric-oracle labels and finite aligned 3D positions, selects and verifies the recorded Sionna/Mitsuba backend, loads the exact referenced scene assets, and renders their triangle meshes with the saved TX/RX samples. It groups panels by scene and transmitter position, allowing a future multiple-TX artifact, and writes image/code/data/runtime hashes plus mesh counts in a provenance sidecar. The renderer was run on `outputs/mvp-six-scene-dmrs-observations-20261022.npz`: 240 points, 40 per scene, with 20 oracle-LOS and 20 oracle-NLOS links per scene. It confirms that this pilot has one fixed TX per scene and sampled RX x-y locations at a fixed height. The plotted meshes are actual scene assets; the figure does not overlay traced ray polylines and does not validate materials or path mechanisms. Class quotas and the LOS-multipath acceptance rule mean the dataset is a selected pipeline pilot, not a representative deployment distribution or powered test set. The PNG and provenance sidecar remain local under ignored `outputs/`; source renderer and optional plotting dependency are tracked. A synchronization command that omitted the Sionna extra removed the local CUDA/Sionna packages; the runtime was restored and verified as PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128 with CUDA available on the RTX PRO 500 Blackwell Generation Laptop GPU, Sionna/RT 2.2.0. The generic lock does not select a CUDA-specific Torch wheel; preserve or separately restore the intended backend after syncing extras.

**Edition 3.106 native RT test initialization and complete local gate:** Fixed pytest collection order so Sionna RT registers its Mitsuba LLVM radio-material plugin before PHY tests import the top-level Sionna package. Without this, eight native RT tests failed with a missing `itu-radio-material` plugin or Dr.Jit variant mismatch; initializing the selected LLVM variant before collection resolves the failures. The full local suite passes: 478 passed, 3 skipped, 88.16% coverage in 396.45 seconds, including the mock integration and all eight native blockage/diffuse, RT-to-NR, and trajectory tests. `uv lock --check`, scoped Ruff lint and format checks, mypy (138 source files), `uv build`, and `git diff --check` also pass. Hosted CI run [39704656804](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39704656804) passed all four jobs on commit `4cbf066ea8e717213370f17a353a48d098348599`. This verifies the current local code and pinned CPU/LLVM RT path, not GPU OptiX availability or the remaining research/physical-validation phases.

**Edition 3.107 absolute-delay CFR and same-path decomposition correction (Phase 0, partial):** Pinned Sionna 2.2 `Paths.cir()` source confirms `normalize_delays=True` subtracts the first valid delay before applying carrier phase, removing the absolute baseband-frequency delay slope. Changed the RT dataset adapter to pass `normalize_delays=False` explicitly, retain `normalize=False`, and version new generation metadata as `loslab.raytraced_generation_report.v2` with an explicit `loslab.rt_absolute_channel.v1` semantic contract. Added a validity-checked zero-interaction path mask that derives direct and indirect CFR components from the **same** `Paths` object; the generator now uses that partition instead of subtracting a separately solved direct channel. A native LLVM 10 m free-space regression compares `rt_cfr()` against the pinned absolute-delay `Paths.cfr()` result and confirms that normalization removes a measurable frequency slope. A native two-ray regression verifies complex full = direct + indirect conservation and residual-power consistency on the same path set. Focused adapter, mocked-generator, and native integration tests pass with coverage disabled for this partial-suite invocation. Old NPZs, DMRS views, and fitted results were not overwritten or relabeled; they remain identifiable legacy runs under their original source hashes and require regeneration before canonical absolute-delay use. Full path-array/API, MIMO partition, gain/link-budget, synchronization-observability, full-suite, and release gates remain open; these corrections alone do not pass G2 or authorize dataset scaling.

**Edition 3.108 physics-correction verification:** The complete local repository suite passes with 481 passed, 3 skipped, and 88.12% coverage; the focused RT contract suite passes all 21 adapter, mocked-generator, native LLVM delay/phase, same-path SISO and explicit 2×3 MIMO, material-sensitivity, and native generation tests. Ruff lint and formatting, mypy (139 configured source files), `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. The full suite started before a final change that also marks masked paths invalid in the copied Sionna object; the focused native suite was rerun and passed after that change. These are local source checks only: no hosted CI ran, no canonical datasets were regenerated, and the remaining Phase 0/UMCD gates are open.

**Edition 3.109 baseband-bin and multipath coefficient verification:** Extended the pinned LLVM RT-to-NR tests to odd as well as even FFT size and compare frequency coordinates and CFR against the explicit CIR/delay Fourier sum. A native two-path test independently reconstructs the absolute-delay CFR from raw `Paths.a` and `Paths.tau`, including Sionna's carrier-phase conversion; the pinned single-precision implementation requires a small absolute tolerance. All 21 focused adapter/mock/native integration tests, Ruff lint/format, and mypy pass locally. Hosted CI run [39711191556](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39711191556) passes all four software jobs for commit `00928a3`; its workflow does not install Sionna RT, so the native tests are locally verified only. This strengthens the baseband mapping and coefficient audit but does not close delay-reference observability, gain/link-budget and polarization qualification, geometry/outage labels, unified dataset layers, or canonical regeneration gates.

**Edition 3.110 Friis gain and phase check:** The native 10 m isotropic direct-path fixture now checks the absolute path-coefficient magnitude against `λ/(4πd)` and its phase against the pinned Sionna Friis reference, alongside `τ=d/c`. The 21 focused adapter/mock/native integration tests pass locally; Ruff lint/format and mypy pass. Hosted CI run 39711191556 validates the previous code revision but does not install native Sionna RT; the new assertion is pending hosted software CI. This verifies a free-space isotropic link budget at one geometry only. Receiver noise/normalization, antenna orientation/polarization, calibrated materials and broader gain convergence remain open.

**Edition 3.111 empty-path and array-mask correction:** Native empty `Paths` tests exposed two failures in same-path partitioning: Dr.Jit cannot select a scalar zero into a zero-length coefficient tensor, and a synthetic-array path mask must broadcast over physical antenna axes instead of matching their expanded shape exactly. `_mask_path_coefficients()` now returns a copy for an empty path axis and broadcasts a validated synthetic mask across the coefficient axes. Native LLVM tests verify finite all-zero CFRs for empty synthetic and explicit-array path sets, and verify direct/indirect conservation for 2×3 arrays in both modes. The full local suite passes at 88.11% coverage; all 24 focused adapter/mock/native tests, Ruff lint/format, and mypy pass. Hosted CI run [39712795569](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39712795569) passes all four software jobs for commit `70421a6`; the workflow does not install Sionna RT, so native behavior is verified locally. Multi-polarization, deeper interactions, link-budget propagation through receiver/noise assumptions, and the remaining canonical-data gates are still open.

**Edition 3.112 polarized MIMO path partition:** Parameterized the native 2×3 direct/indirect conservation test across synthetic and explicit arrays and all TX/RX V/VH combinations. All eight native LLVM cases pass and preserve the complete antenna-pair axis count; together with the existing empty-path and adapter tests, all 30 focused adapter/mock/native tests pass. Ruff lint/format and mypy pass. Hosted CI run [39713206686](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39713206686) passes all four software jobs for commit `1a1de6c`; the workflow omits native Sionna RT. This checks tensor shape and coherent same-path partitioning under Sionna's supported polarization-port layouts; it does not validate cross-polarization coefficients against independent gold values, antenna orientation realism, calibrated arrays, or multipath distributions.

**Edition 3.113 spatially disjoint TX/RX holdout and reproducible 3D scene view:** Re-ran the City43 synthetic 4×4 scene pilot with west, center, and east transmitter positions and corresponding receiver disks of 25 m radius centered 80 m apart. Each domain contains 20 geometric-LOS and 20 geometric-NLOS links; all 120 Sionna PUSCH-DMRS observations use the same precomputed input. The summary auditor now verifies that every RX coordinate lies within its declared disk, requires the exact disks to be pairwise disjoint, reports their geometric separation and sample-level nearest-RX diagnostics, and preserves the joint TX/RX shift semantics. The exact disk gaps are 30/30/110 m; the minimum cross-domain sampled-RX distances are 33.89/33.99/114.98 m, with no pair within 1 m. This is a within-one-scene location-shift smoke pilot, not a TX-only causal test or independent-scene evaluation. Descriptive three-location AUROC means are 0.622 logistic regression, 0.746 random forest, and 0.573 raw MLP, with 40 balanced test links per fold, only 7 training seeds, and 7–11 spatial groups per fold; these numbers do not support a benchmark or method-superiority claim. The audited report is [`rt-urban43-joint-location-holdout-20261022.json`](results/rt-urban43-joint-location-holdout-20261022.json), SHA256 `bd7e94b78defca25ab4d73d67ba09dba5697656a20e78093fdbde2ccb81407ce`; source NPZs and per-sample predictions remain in ignored `outputs/rt-urban43-tx-spatial-holdout-20261022-v3/`. [`plot_rt_tx_spatial_holdout.py`](../../scripts/plot_rt_tx_spatial_holdout.py) renders the actual scene manifest's building geometry together with all TX/RX positions; its PNG is committed under `figures/`. The scene and material model are synthetic and unqualified, labels remain geometric direct-path oracles, and no measured-radio inference is made.

Native Sionna RT is tested locally on LLVM and remains absent from hosted CI.

**Edition 3.115 end-to-end TE/TM slab-reflection coefficient check:** Added a native pinned-Sionna test on the built-in planar reflector with a declared dielectric slab (`εr=4`, conductivity `0.01 S/m`, thickness `0.1 m`) at 3.5 GHz and incidence angle 45°. The test computes independent textbook complex TE/TM slab coefficients, then normalizes the measured reflected `Paths.a` coefficient by the direct-path coefficient and geometric path-length ratio. V/V matches TM and H/H matches TE; direct gain matches Friis and both delays match image-source geometry. Both LLVM cases pass. This is an end-to-end validation of Sionna's polarization basis and slab coefficient in one controlled scene, not calibration of any real building material or a general coefficient/convergence validation.

**Edition 3.115 local verification:** The complete repository suite passes with 627 passed, 3 skipped, and 88.12% coverage (630 collected tests). Ruff check/format, mypy across 140 source files, `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted CI [run 39719682064](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39719682064) passed all four software jobs for `498082f`; the hosted workflow does not install native Sionna RT.

**Edition 3.116 absolute-power NR receiver mode:** Added an opt-in RT→PUSCH/DMRS observation mode with explicit average transmit power per active resource element and fixed thermal noise `k_B T B F`, using subcarrier spacing as the noise bandwidth. Unlike the existing normalized-SNR diagnostic mode, it preserves absolute channel gain through the LS estimate and records transmit power, noise figure, temperature, bandwidth, and noise variance in the generation report. GPU-backed Sionna PHY regression confirms a half-amplitude channel remains half amplitude in this mode, while the normalized mode produces identical observations for those two channel gains under the same seed. The ideal-synchronization and flat-frequency-grid limits remain; this is not an end-to-end waveform/link-budget qualification.

**Edition 3.116 verification:** The complete local suite passes with 88.14% coverage, including the CUDA Sionna PHY gain-preservation regression and analytic thermal-noise checks. Ruff check/format across 224 maintained files, mypy across 140 source files, `uv lock --check`, and `uv build` pass. Hosted CI [run 39721406035](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39721406035) passes all four software jobs. Hosted CI does not install native Sionna RT or exercise the CUDA-only receiver regression.

**Edition 3.117 UMCD schema foundation:** Added validated multi-node episode records with distinct radiating elements, antenna ports, RF chains, polarization and complex port-to-element weights; directed links carry explicit ordered port maps. Added a CSR-style ragged path-oracle table with absolute delays, complex coefficients, optional Doppler/angles, named solve outcomes (including no-path versus solver failure), per-link named-axis CFR views, and version-hashed target definitions with per-element validity/reason codes. `datasets/episode_store.py` writes episode, path, channel and target layers to separate content-addressed pickle-free objects and publishes the immutable manifest last. Focused dataset tests pass. This is an initial static-schema/storage implementation: receiver-view references/access policy, moving poses, full solver/material provenance, split registry, richer path-attribute validity, schema migration and production-scale Arrow/Zarr storage remain open; no canonical dataset is released by this change.

**Edition 3.117 verification:** The complete repository test suite passes at 87.77% coverage, including the new episode-store round-trip, hash-integrity, and schema validation cases. Ruff check/format across 230 maintained files, mypy across 145 source files, `uv lock --check`, and `uv build` pass. Hosted CI [run 39723486090](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39723486090) passes all four jobs; hosted CI does not install native Sionna RT or validate production-scale storage performance.

**Edition 3.118 native path-truth adapter:** Added `integrations/sionna/path_oracle.py` to map a selected pinned Sionna 2.2 TX/RX device pair into UMCD's ragged per-link/frame/antenna-port path table. The converter keeps valid complex coefficients and absolute delays, derives path interaction order and kind from Sionna interaction slots, and carries per-path Doppler and AoA/AoD with explicit azimuth/elevation conversion. Native LLVM tests compare each field against the original `Paths` arrays for synthetic/explicit 2×3 arrays and V/VH polarizations; they also exercise no-path outcomes and selecting a specific pair from a multi-TX/multi-RX scene. This implements the first field-extraction part of Stage S2. Native object/primitive IDs and vertices, episode-level link enumeration, geometry-derived LOS visibility, solver convergence and path-budget convergence remain unimplemented.

**Edition 3.118 verification:** The complete local suite passes with 518 passed, 3 skipped, and 87.75% coverage. Eleven focused native LLVM path-oracle cases pass across both array modes, all V/VH combinations, empty paths, and multi-device link selection. Ruff check/format pass across 231 maintained files; mypy passes across 146 source files; `uv lock --check` and `uv build` pass. Hosted CI [run 39725668192](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39725668192) passes all four jobs. Hosted CI does not install native Sionna RT, so the path-oracle physics checks are local-only.

**Edition 3.119 interaction geometry trace:** Extended the UMCD ragged path table with a second CSR offset level for each path's ordered interactions, preserving native interaction-type flags, scene-local object and primitive IDs, and Sionna scene-XYZ vertices in meters. The native adapter handles synthetic and explicit array axes and filters padded `NONE` depths without fabricating interaction rows. The immutable episode store persists these arrays and the declared vertex coordinate frame; validation rejects partial traces, offset/order disagreement, and invalid coordinates. Native LLVM comparisons and bundle round-trip tests pass. This captures interaction geometry from the path solver; object IDs remain scene-realization scoped, and this does not replace an independent geometric direct-visibility oracle or solver-convergence validation.

**Edition 3.119 verification:** The complete local suite passes with 519 passed, 3 skipped, and 87.77% coverage. Thirteen focused dataset/native cases cover interaction-trace validation, storage round-trip, synthetic/explicit arrays, V/VH polarization, zero paths, and multi-device selection. Hosted CI [run 39727460346](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39727460346) passes all four jobs. Hosted workflows do not install native Sionna RT.
**Edition 3.120 independent geometric LOS oracle:** RT generation now labels links
from an independent finite Mitsuba scene-segment query, cross-checked against
Sionna's depth-zero LOS-only solver. Four parallel probes detect boundary-
sensitive links and a grazing-incidence threshold marks tangent hits ambiguous.
Ambiguous links and geometry/solver disagreements are counted and skipped;
per-link records retain segment length, first-hit distance, incidence cosine,
and probe outcomes. Native LLVM tests cover clear, screen-blocked, and screen-
edge links; mock tests verify that mismatches never enter datasets. This
fixture-level gate does not settle material penetration or broader orientation
and coplanar cases. Historical datasets retain their existing provenance and
were not rewritten. The City43 3D figure now gives building meshes/footprints
more contrast and shows three TX locations with sampled RX links.
**Edition 3.120 verification:** The full local suite passes with 522 passed, 3
skipped, and 87.75% coverage. Ruff check and formatting pass across the 232-file
maintained scope; mypy passes across 147 configured source files; `uv lock
--check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted CI is pending. Native
Mitsuba/Sionna RT coverage is verified on this local LLVM backend; hosted CI
does not install RT.
**Edition 3.121 whole-episode RT path mapping:** Added
`integrations/sionna/rt_network.py` to assemble the `Paths` outputs for every
link and frame in an UMCD episode. Bindings explicitly identify Sionna device
indices; link port maps can select and reorder array ports, and the resulting
table must cover each link/frame/port pair exactly. Empty solves and solver
failure/unconverged outcomes remain represented. A native LLVM 2-TX×2-RX test
maps all four device pairs into one episode; unit tests cover port-map ordering,
missing/duplicate bindings, and failed-solve groups. Scene-to-episode array
extraction and broad physical verification remain open.
**Edition 3.121 verification:** The complete local suite passes with 87.71%
coverage (configured minimum: 75%); the new RT-network tests pass on the native
LLVM backend. Ruff check and formatting pass across the 234-file maintained

`git diff --check` passes. Hosted CI remains to be checked after pushing; it
does not install native Sionna RT.
**Edition 3.122 static scene-to-episode extraction:** Added
`integrations/sionna/array_layouts.py` to convert the pinned Sionna 2.2 scene
device mappings, positions, orientations, normalized element locations,
pattern-major feed ordering, and carrier frequency into UMCD arrays, nodes,
and directed links. It preserves Sionna TX/RX device-axis order and supplies
ready-to-use `SionnaPathBinding` records for shared or per-link `Paths`
results. Multi-port RF-chain counts and physical pattern labels must be
provided by the caller; unknown network roles default to `OTHER`; moving
devices are rejected until pose timelines are represented. A native LLVM
2-TX×2-RX, dual-polarized, two-element test assembles all 64 link/frame/feed
groups and checks delay mapping against the native solver output. No material
or RF-chain calibration is implied.

the static extraction suite passes including native Sionna RT. Ruff check and

**Edition 3.123 geometry visualization and controlled-link convergence diagnostic:** The spatial companion figure [`rt-urban43-spatial-holdout-20261022.png`](figures/rt-urban43-spatial-holdout-20261022.png) shows the synthetic City43 4×4 building layout in 3D and top-down views, three TX positions, and 120 sampled RX links (20 geometric LOS and 20 geometric NLOS in each of three disjoint RX regions). These are synthetic axis-aligned building volumes, not surveyed city meshes or measured propagation. The convergence figure [`rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-20261022.png`](figures/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-20261022.png) is a separate solver diagnostic for a thin screen; it intentionally has no building scene and must be read alongside the spatial figure, not as a scene-layout image.

The convergence runner now supports paired geometry-labeled LOS and geometric-NLOS links in one scene and records segment-visibility evidence, absolute-delay CFR configuration, path counts, delay spread, and angular spread. On the controlled `box_one_screen` pair, ray-budget changes from 20k to 100k at depth 7 were relatively small for the reported aggregate observables, but cross-seed CFR and path-count differences remain. Increasing depth from 3 through 7 materially changes path counts, CFR, delay spread, and angular spread. Therefore sample-budget stability at this one synthetic scene does not establish depth convergence: gate G3.01 remains open. The 100k result is a comparison reference, not ground truth. Older convergence JSON metadata claiming delay normalization is inconsistent with the runner's actual absolute-delay CFR call; the historical reports were left unchanged and are superseded for this controlled pair by the schema-v2 reports in `docs/research/results/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence*20261022.json`.

**Edition 3.123 verification:** The complete local suite passes at 87.69% coverage, including native LLVM Sionna tests. Maintained-scope Ruff check/format, mypy across 150 source files, `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. GitHub Actions [run 39739420589](https://github.com/nntk072/5g-nr-channel-condition/actions/runs/39739420589) passes all four software jobs; hosted CI does not install Mitsuba, so the explicitly marked native RT link-matrix test is skipped there and executed locally.

**Edition 3.124 max-depth-9 convergence follow-up (negative):** Extended the same matched `box_one_screen` visible LOS and geometric-NLOS links to max depth 9 at 100k samples/source, seeds 11/23/47, on the native LLVM backend. Both link states are independently reclassified by the segment-visibility oracle. Depth 8→9 raises LOS valid-path counts from 802–804 to 1,223–1,226, RMS delay spread from about 40.2 ns to 45.6 ns, and angular spread from about 53.5° to 55.0°. For geometric NLOS, path counts rise from 482–483 to 852–855, delay spread from about 35.0 ns to 42.3 ns, and angular spread from about 83.8° to 85.0°. Against the depth-9 CFR reference, depth-8 relative L2 error is about 0.29 for LOS and 0.11–0.14 for NLOS. Thus the depth trend remains material through 9; G3.01 remains open, and none of these finite-depth/finite-budget outputs is ground truth. The added report is `docs/research/results/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-depth9-20261022.json`; the updated comparison plot includes it.

**Edition 3.125 diffuse-material audit and sample-budget diagnostic:** Runtime inspection of the pinned Sionna 2.2 `box_one_screen` scene found `scattering_coefficient=0` on both `box-mat` and `screen-mat`; the scene XML omits the property and Sionna's ITU-material default is zero. Therefore the v2 max-depth-3–9 reports and their `diffuse_reflection=True` flag do not establish diffuse scattering. Their depth trend is still evidence for the combined direct/specular/refraction solve, but their `samples_per_src` curves must be described generically as PathSolver ray-sample sensitivity, not a diffuse-ray convergence result.

The convergence runner is now schema v3: it records every material's scattering/XPD coefficients and scattering-pattern class, the explicit `max_num_paths_per_src` cap, and valid diffuse versus non-diffuse path counts, power, delay spread, and angular spread. The native LLVM regression compares the scene default S=0 with a deliberately synthetic screen override S=0.5. At depth 3 and 1k samples, the LOS case goes from 48 total/0 diffuse paths to 103 total/55 diffuse paths; the blocked case goes from 32/0 to 37/5. This validates that the pinned solver emits paths marked as diffuse when a nonzero material coefficient is present; S=0.5 is an API/mechanism fixture, not a calibrated building value.

The S=0.5 sample sweep (depth 3, seeds 11/23/47, 200/2k/20k/100k samples/source, 1M path cap) shows why sampled diffuse rays must not be counted as fixed physical paths. Median LOS diffuse-path count rises from 21 to 5,564 and blocked-link count from 2 to 134. The non-diffuse counts remain 48 and 32 respectively. LOS diffuse power and delay summaries are more stable by 20k–100k than the blocked-link diffuse delay/angular summaries; the 20k-to-100k median CFR error is about 0.119 LOS and 0.078 NLOS, so no global sample-budget tolerance is passed. The component-separated report is `docs/research/results/rt-box-los-nlos-diffuse-budget-sweep-s05-components-20261022.json`, with the [diffuse-budget figure](figures/rt-box-los-nlos-diffuse-budget-s05-20261022.png). These are one synthetic scene/link family results; material calibration, other geometries, depth convergence with nonzero scattering, and path-cap stress remain open. G3.01, G3.02, and G3.03 remain open; G3.04 has a controlled-fixture demonstration but is not yet generalized.

**Edition 3.126 nonzero-scattering interaction-depth follow-up (negative convergence result):** Completed a 48-run depth sweep on the same paired LOS/geometric-NLOS links with `screen-mat` explicitly set to S=0.5, depths 0–6, seeds 11/23/47, 20k/100k samples per source, and a 1,000,000-path cap. At depth 6 and 100k samples, median valid-path counts are 10,846 (10,748–10,850) for LOS, including 10,546 diffuse paths, and 3,539 (3,473–3,560) for blocked NLOS, including 3,358 diffuse paths. Relative complex-CFR L2 error against the selected depth-6/100k reference is still 0.360 at LOS depth 5 and 0.898 at blocked-NLOS depth 5; error is non-monotone across depths. From depth 3 to 6, median power-weighted RMS delay spread rises from 14.27 to 28.92 ns (LOS) and 18.23 to 26.74 ns (NLOS), while arrival angular spread rises from 42.4° to 50.7° and 71.3° to 80.6°, respectively. This does not establish convergence through depth 6 and reinforces that the selected high-depth/high-budget result is only a finite reference, not truth. The run uses an uncalibrated synthetic S=0.5 material override and one thin-screen geometry; G3.01 remains open, and material, geometry, and path-cap gates are not passed. Report: `docs/research/results/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-depth6-s05-20261022.json`; plot: [S=0.5 depth-sensitivity figure](figures/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-depth6-s05-20261022.png).

**Edition 3.126 verification:** The full local suite passes with 547 passed, 3 skipped, and 87.69% coverage. Maintained-scope Ruff lint and formatting pass across 241 Python files, mypy passes across 151 source files, `uv lock --check`, `uv build`, and `git diff --check` pass. The focused convergence/scattering tests pass; this verifies implementation and deterministic fixture behavior on the available CPU/LLVM backend, not physical material calibration or convergence of general scenes.

**Edition 3.127 controlled path-cap stress (partial gate evidence):** At max depth 6, 100k ray samples/source, seeds 11/23/47, and screen S=0.5, matched caps of 1k, 10k, and 1M were compared for the visible and blocked links. At 1k, all LOS seeds return 999 valid paths (993 diffuse), with 5.40 ns RMS delay spread and 29.1° angular spread; the blocked link returns 537–539 paths (487–489 diffuse), 22.05 ns and 73.8°. At 10k, LOS returns 8,210–8,245 paths (8,048–8,087 diffuse), 22.76–23.05 ns and about 47.5°; the blocked-link path count and aggregate metrics match the 1M-cap results for all three seeds. The 1M cap yields 10,748–10,850 LOS paths (10,449–10,550 diffuse), 28.90–28.94 ns and about 50.7°. Thus a 1k cap is clearly inadequate for both links in this fixture; 10k still changes the LOS result substantially, even though its returned count is below 10k. The 10k result does not establish a general safe cap: this is one synthetic geometry/material, one sample budget/depth, and no cross-cap CFR-vector comparison is available in the compact convergence reports. Gate G3.05 remains open pending cap sweeps across scene families, depth/budget combinations, and direct numerical channel comparisons. Reports: `docs/research/results/rt-box-los-nlos-convergence-depth6-s05-cap1000-20261022.json` and `...cap10000-20261022.json`; compact comparison: `docs/research/figures/rt-box-los-nlos-path-cap-sweep-s05-20261022.json` and [figure](figures/rt-box-los-nlos-path-cap-sweep-s05-20261022.png).

**Edition 3.128 corrected the City43 spatial figure's label legend:** The generator defines binary label 1 as clear geometric LOS and 0 as blocked geometric NLOS. The plot had these names/colors reversed even though the stored labels and experiment targets were correct. An independent pinned-LLVM segment-visibility audit rechecked all 120 saved links: 60 LOS/visible, 60 NLOS/blocked, zero mismatches, zero ambiguous. The regenerated [3D building and link figure](figures/rt-urban43-spatial-holdout-20261022.png) now maps colors and names to that contract; a sidecar records dataset, scene-manifest, and output hashes. The reusable auditor and full per-link result are `scripts/audit_rt_spatial_holdout_labels.py` and `docs/research/results/rt-urban43-spatial-label-audit-20261022.json`. This corrects visualization only; it does not upgrade synthetic geometry to measured truth.
